Development and decision log

This is the running, append-only human record for the standalone Market Analogues project. Each material milestone records what changed, why it was chosen, how it was implemented or measured, what failed, and what evidence controls the next decision.

Logging rule: add an entry before each material checkpoint commit. Never rewrite a failed result into a pass; later entries may supersede a decision while preserving the original reasoning.

Decision register

IDDecisionReasonRevisit when
D-001Keep this as a standalone OHLCV project.The engine must work on NASDAQ, NSE, or any canonical OHLCV source without coupling to either parent repository.Do not revisit unless the input contract itself changes.
D-002Retrieve historical analogues; do not classify named chart patterns.The desired question is “what prior paths resemble the current development, and what followed?” Named labels discard uncertainty and morphology.Named labels may be added only as post-retrieval explanations.
D-003Use causal, explainable multichannel representations.A professional chart reading includes OHLC movement, stages, volatility/contraction, volume/shocks, market regime, relative strength and structural turns.Any new channel must pass causality, invariance and ablation tests.
D-004Keep exact composite distance as the authority.One cheap weighted proxy hid good matches in individual views. Candidate generation may approximate; final ranking must remain explainable.Only replace after a common-oracle comparison proves a better metric.
D-005Use observed trading sessions for the history gap.Business-day estimates can disagree with exchange sessions and admit temporally invalid candidates.Never; this is a correctness invariant.
D-006Do not promote multiview to the default yet.Sampled Gate 10 passes, but full-universe pool saturation and raw-on-query latency fail.After persisted shards, exact-safe pruning, and independent Gate 12 pass.
D-007Next build persisted view shards, then exact-safe pruning.Full-universe runs show bounded memory but repeated raw feature computation and approximate shortlist instability. More hand-tuned weights cannot prove completeness.Re-evaluate after Gate 11 differential and performance evidence.
D-008Keep LB_Keogh optional; use the non-DTW partial sum by default.Across all Gate 09 cases LB_Keogh avoids more exact work, but aggregate scoring time is slightly better on NSE and slightly worse on NASDAQ. Extra bound work is not a universal latency win.After a calibrated cost policy can predict when the stronger bound repays its overhead.
D-009Expose persisted exact-safe search as an opt-in production backend.Repeated complete 252-bar store searches pass determinism, latency and memory budgets, but only one current query per exchange has been locked outside Gate 09.Make it the default only after the independent Gate 12 matrix passes.
D-010Build the exhaustive authority as two-pass streaming, not a full exact-feature store.The lossless native layout needs float32 coarse plus float64 exact fields and projects to 52.81 GiB; reduced precision changes authoritative values, while only 9.47 GiB is free and 5 GiB is reserved.Only revisit after the exact representation contract or available storage changes and the feasibility gate is rerun.
D-011Use behavioral precision contracts: float16 candidates, native reference.Float16 preserved all 24 top-20 sets and ≥0.9999925 nDCG, so twelve-decimal equality is unnecessary for approximate retrieval. It changed one order, is not a safe native lower bound, and still cannot fit as a full store.Revisit if a formal quantization-error bound is proven or storage/resources materially change.
D-012Use trader-labelled examples as a diagnostic benchmark, not ground truth.The sheet is valuable independent evidence, but labels are imbalanced, symbol coverage is partial, and daily bars cannot reproduce the intraday trigger. Outcomes must stay outside retrieval.Revisit label-specific conclusions after broader venue/delisted coverage and a preregistered full-universe walk-forward audit.
D-013Keep 75% as a visible target; never tune the audit until it passes.The broader Yahoo result fails the target: top-1 is indistinguishable from random, while top-5 has only a four-point lift concentrated in breakouts. Changing labels, cutoffs or weights after observing it would convert evaluation into calibration.Only evaluate a new trigger-aware representation on a separately locked holdout.
D-014Freeze daily long-swing case memory before new outcome evaluation.Historical analogue evidence is vulnerable to changing decision times, horizons, barriers, diversity rules and abstention after seeing results. A digest-bound contract and empty append-only trial ledger make later changes visible.Create a new version and ledger entry; never rewrite daily-long-swing-v1.
D-015Define M01's present universe as configured source instruments, not exchange membership.No dated membership, delisting-return or permanent-identity tables accompany the sources. Treating the directory as the complete historical exchange would silently introduce survivorship and identity assumptions.Expand the boundary only after a separately fingerprinted point-in-time master is ingested and reconciled.
D-016Add M02 as a non-breaking state layer with explicit masks.The verified production representation is single-lookback and its coarse vector substitutes zero for missing values. Replacing it before a common verifier would invalidate Gate 12; a separate six-resolution layer can retain full channels and distinguish missing from neutral before M03 comparison.Integrate a new distance only after M03 structural verification and later Gate 12 production-reference comparison pass.
D-017Retain the failed M03 v1 trial and require a new holdout for any correction.The overall averages passed, but family 04 precision@5 and the total runtime failed their locked limits. Tuning or rerunning the same seeds would turn the verifier into a training set.Develop from the generator contract and disjoint development seeds, preregister a changed distance and untouched validation seeds, then execute it once.
D-018Adopt the M03b topology blend for production comparison, not direct production promotion.A minimal 20% multi-scale topology term corrects V1's rigid-shape confusion and passes a fresh synthetic holdout. Synthetic separation alone cannot prove full-universe nearest-neighbour recall on real charts.M04 must compare it with every frozen Gate 12 exact authority and enforce diversity, temporal validity and resource budgets before integration.
D-019Use certified composite top-1,000 as the non-displacing proposal core; component routes are additive seeds.Compact-only proposal-v2 failed recall and a separate compact store violates the 11 GiB cap. The actual packed quantized bound retains all 240 development targets within rank 343. Component bounds already present in the pack add coverage without being mistaken for completeness certificates.Change only after a common certified-completion bake-off proves a different proposal lowers end-to-end latency without changing any result or certificate.
D-020Treat CPU, RAM and disk as measured optimization resources, not fixed M04R-09 rejection caps.The user prioritizes exact correctness and best measured latency. A variant may use more resources when it is faster and reliable; actual allocation failure, operational instability or irreproducibility still fails it.Reintroduce a hard deployment cap only when a real target environment requires one.
D-021Deprioritize ANN as an exact-completion optimization; optimize the certified bound scan first.A complete 24-query audit proves 97.62% of current exact rows are forced by admissible bounds. Even a perfect zero-cost proposer projects to only 1.69% complete-pipeline savings and cannot improve any heavy-tail query.Revisit ANN after scan parallelism/caching, or if a proposer can accelerate a separately labelled non-certified interactive mode.
D-022Select eight spawned one-thread, retrieval-time-balanced query groups for certified batches.It saturates all eight logical CPUs, keeps proposals local to each process, reproduces every frozen result/certificate and reduces complete critical-path time by 75.37%. Four processes × two threads and serial shared scan are slower.Rebenchmark when host topology, pack layout or query count changes; retain the deterministic serial path as the reference fallback.
D-023Freeze 60 untouched NASDAQ cases with benchmark-only historical stratification.Five symbols per quality/liquidity cell exceeds the preferred 50-case target. Benchmark regime and era can guarantee temporal diversity without using stock outcomes; morphology is measured only after the one final selection. Causal prefixes permit append-only source growth.Never retune this registry from M04R-11 results. Add a separately versioned registry only for a preregistered new hypothesis or a newly discovered identity alias.

L-001 — Standalone market-agnostic foundation

What: Created canonical CSV/Parquet directory and long-table adapters, quality audit, causal episode construction, benchmark alignment, representations, exact distance, outcomes and HTML reporting.

Why: Existing NASDAQ/NSE repositories are data providers, not a suitable home for a reusable analogue engine. Source data must remain read-only and derived artifacts isolated.

How: Normalized the input contract to timestamp/open/high/low/close/volume plus symbol, used dataset configuration rather than market-specific branches, and wrote artifacts beneath a configured output directory.

Verification: Adapter canonicalization, quality rules, scale invariance, causal episode boundaries, context alignment and report tests.

L-002 — Research and prior-art incorporation

What: Compared chart-pattern literature, time-series similarity papers, DTW/ShapeDTW, matrix-profile/MASS approaches, public stock matchers, aeon, tslearn and UCR baselines.

Why: Reuse validated ideas and avoid rebuilding known methods without knowing their limitations.

How: Adopted streaming candidate generation, optional MASS acceleration, bounded DTW, overlap exclusion, structural events, method comparison and outcome summaries. Kept generic time-series baselines separate from the financial metamorphic oracle.

Decision: No inspected repository supplied the complete requested combination of professional-chart context, causal controls, exact reranking, outcome separation and market-agnostic ingestion. Ideas were incorporated without copying incompatible code.

Detailed sources and comparisons remain in research evidence and repository comparison.

L-003 — Verified exact metric baseline

What: Locked a composite distance over coarse, stage, price/DTW, candle-volatility, volume-shock, market-context and structural components.

Why: The representation must preserve the stock’s development and market context while remaining price-unit and volume-unit invariant.

How: Precomputed resampled representation arrays, stage and structural signatures; reused exact scores through selection; kept forward outcomes strictly outside retrieval.

Evidence: Synthetic retrieval passes at recall@10 0.953846 and nDCG@10 0.935744 with zero critical-negative errors and zero future-mutation delta.

Rejected: Simple metric reweighting and a single dense weighted proxy reduced synthetic or candidate evidence, so the stable metric was restored.

L-004 — Complete-universe baseline measurement

What: Added deterministic serial/parallel scans, explicit failure accounting, coverage reconciliation, memory/time metrics and pool-saturation reports.

Why: Passing unit tests or a few symbols cannot establish complete-universe behavior.

How: Scanned every quality-eligible symbol at stride five and reranked a bounded pool. Used observed query sessions for the 60-bar gap.

Evidence: NSE scanned 1,843 instruments and 1,040,155 windows in 92.61 s at 436 MB. NASDAQ scanned 11,584 instruments and 3,786,156 windows in 203.49 s at 453 MB. Zero eligible-symbol failures.

Failure retained: pool-500 recall versus pool 1,000 was only 0.75 NSE and 0.65 NASDAQ; coarse/exact Spearman was 0.145 and 0.080. Gate 08 remains failed.

L-005 — Stratified exhaustive sampled oracle

What: Built Gate 09 to exactly score every candidate in deterministic market/quality/liquidity/time samples and persist full component rankings.

Why: Comparing pool 500 to pool 1,000 only measures saturation against another approximate pool. A locked exact target is required to reason about recall.

How: Measured recent median dollar volume for all A/B instruments; selected deterministic low/middle/high strata; sampled episodes across eligible history; scored all with the exact composite; explicitly marked post-overlap-deduplication oracle membership/rank; persisted digests and Parquet rankings.

Evidence: NSE 12/12 cases, 3,192 episodes, 24 stratified instruments. NASDAQ 12/12 cases, 2,658 episodes, 18 stratified instruments. Zero temporal, duplicate, non-finite or top-k failures.

L-006 — Deterministic multiview fusion

What: Added independent coarse, price-shape, 12-stage development, candle-volatility, volume-shock, market-context and structural candidate views; local per-symbol quotas; stable reciprocal-rank fusion; cached/versioned oracle view distances; and a reusable Gate 10 command/report.

Why: Raw distances have incompatible scales, and one weighted proxy can bury a strong match visible in only one professional-chart view.

How: Converted each smaller-is-better view to deterministic ranks, guaranteed view representation, deduplicated candidates, then exact-reranked. Stable candidate IDs break all ties. Serial/parallel and shuffled-input tests assert determinism.

Iterations: Cheap v1 failed badly: only 1/12 NSE and 3/12 NASDAQ at pool 125, worst recall 0.60/0.75. v2 added a multichannel coarse view and aligned stage development with the exact 12-stage structure. At pool 125 it still failed 4 NSE and 1 NASDAQ cases. Pool 175 is the first tested pool to clear 0.95 in every sampled case.

Evidence: Gate 10 passes 12/12 per market at a worst-case recall of 0.95. Failed attempts remain under gate history.

L-007 — Full-universe multiview smoke and stop decision

What: Ran one 252-bar current query over each complete quality-eligible universe with multiview scanning and exact reranking of pool 1,000. Runs executed concurrently and wrote separate smoke reports without overwriting Gate 08.

Why: A calibrated sampled pass must not be promoted without whole-market fidelity and performance evidence.

Evidence: NSE: 1,843 instruments, 1,040,155 windows, 130.56 s scan, 311.71 s total, 458 MB peak, pool-175 versus pool-1,000 recall 0.40. NASDAQ: 11,584 instruments, 3,786,156 windows, 791.27 s scan, 902.61 s total, 757 MB peak, recall 0.50. Both had zero scan/build failures.

Interpretation: Memory and coverage are sound. Latency and shortlist saturation fail. Pool 1,000 is not exhaustive, so even these recall numbers are not completeness guarantees.

Decision: Keep multiview opt-in. Stop approximate weight tuning. Proceed to persisted view shards and mathematically safe progressive exact scoring before independent Gate 12.

L-008 — Initial Git checkpoint

What: Added this running log and prepared the complete source, tests, configuration examples and readable plans for the repository’s first commit.

Why: Future work needs both code-level rollback and the reasoning needed to understand why a previous approach passed, failed or was rejected.

Scope: Generated market data, large oracle Parquets, reports, gates, virtual environments and caches remain ignored. Their compact metrics and decisions are recorded here; they are reproducible from source and local datasets.

L-009 — Gate 11A persisted-view foundation and NSE 252 layer

What: Added a fixed 240-value, query-independent signature contract; atomic per-symbol compressed NPZ shards; schema/signature/storage versions; source fingerprints; content and manifest digests; deterministic layered manifests; stale-input invalidation; corruption errors and opt-in rebuild; resumable serial/parallel builds; and warm persisted candidate search.

Why: The full-universe multiview smoke recomputed seven views from raw OHLCV for every query. It was memory-safe but took 130.56 seconds on NSE and 791.27 seconds on NASDAQ before exact reranking.

How: Extracted close path, range/body, robust volume/shock, market/relative path, 12-stage development and structural features into fixed-size vectors. Vectorized all sliding-window construction per symbol. Stores use stable episode IDs and cutoff nanoseconds; queries load only matching lookback/quality shards, enforce observed-session and same-symbol overlap limits, take local per-view candidates, then apply deterministic reciprocal-rank fusion.

Correctness: Vectorized signatures match the episode reference within 2e-5. Batch and single distances agree. Float16 storage preserves every tested per-view top-10 set and stays within 0.5% / 0.005 tolerance. Tests cover round-trip, serial/parallel digest identity, incremental lookback merge, unchanged resume, corruption detection, explicit repair, safe manifest paths and deterministic temporal search.

Optimization evidence: The same 1,264-row smoke store fell from 10.56 seconds to 0.31 seconds after vectorization and kept the exact same pre-quantization content digest. Float16 reduced a 125,907-row scale sample from 95 MB to 52 MB.

Full NSE 252 evidence: 1,843/1,843 eligible shards, 1,061,384 rows, 262 quality exclusions, zero failures, 106.64-second initial build, 442 MB disk. An unchanged run reused all 1,843 shards in 5.21 seconds with manifest digest 52672c6baf48323e045e45c0dd68641ce18acd06b45001a9c7294cec9c9b2b8e. Warm query candidate generation over 1,040,117 eligible rows took 13.63–15.54 seconds and returned pool 1,000, versus 130.56 seconds raw-on-query.

Limits: This checkpoint covers only NSE lookback 252. Persisted candidate generation does not solve the 1,000-candidate exact-rerank cost or prove exhaustive recall. NASDAQ 252, other lookbacks and exact-safe pruning remain open.

L-010 — Full NASDAQ 252 layer, adaptive precision and persisted-oracle gate

What: Extended Gate 11A to the complete NASDAQ 252-bar universe and added a separate oracle mode that evaluates the persisted signature contract rather than assuming the earlier on-demand cheap-v2 result transfers.

Incident: The first full build emitted a float16 overflow warning on an extreme-but-finite signature. Continuing would have produced a rejected shard and failed manifest, so the run was interrupted. Completed shards were atomic and remained reusable.

Fix: Changed compact storage into an adaptive policy: use float16 when all finite values fit; use float32 for only an out-of-range shard; never clip. Added a boundary regression test. Resume ran with corruption-aware repair, reused 8,583 completed shards, built/repaired 3,001 remaining shards, and produced a complete manifest. Exactly one of 11,584 final shards required float32.

Full NASDAQ evidence: 11,584/11,584 eligible shards, 3,823,301 stored rows, 496 quality exclusions, zero failures, 148.12-second resumed completion, 1.6 GB disk. An unchanged validation reused all 11,584 shards in 27.74 seconds with manifest digest 698a4026b4859adac34a3c4ade0dc66b3c84bdbd722fdb0176065f8d7b158d92.

Warm performance: Persisted AAPL retrieval considered 3,786,118 eligible rows and returned pool 1,000 in 58.47–62.48 seconds, versus 791.27 seconds for raw-on-query multiview scanning. The remaining cost is opening 11,584 small files plus exact reranking.

Recall evidence: New signature oracle mode passes 12/12 NSE and 12/12 NASDAQ cases at pool 175 with a 0.95 worst-case top-20 recall floor. Its cached columns are versioned view-signature-v1 and its gate/report are separate from cheap-v2.

Decision: The 252-bar persisted candidate layer is correct enough to proceed to exact-safe scoring. Do not build 63/126 across both exchanges yet: disk has fallen below 10 GB, and exact pruning is now the more important bottleneck. Consolidating small shards is a future I/O optimization, not a prerequisite for Gate 11B correctness.

L-011 — Gate 11B first exact-safe progressive scorer

What: Split exact representation scoring into a non-DTW lower-bound phase and a completion phase. Added a constraint-aware progressive scorer that orders candidates by the safe bound, maintains the current overlap/per-instrument-aware top-k threshold, and skips DTW only when a candidate cannot beat that threshold. Added an optional symmetric multivariate LB_Keogh bound inside the same Sakoe–Chiba band.

Why: Persisted candidate generation is now fast enough, but building representations and exact-reranking pool 1,000 remains the dominant end-to-end cost. All composite weights and component distances are non-negative, allowing a formal partial-sum lower bound without changing the metric.

Safety argument: The default bound includes every exact non-DTW component and 55% rigid price contribution; the omitted DTW contribution is bounded by zero. Candidates are processed in ascending lower-bound/episode-ID order. Once a future bound is strictly greater than the worst currently selected exact match, it cannot enter a full feasible top-k. Strict comparison preserves ties. Optional LB_Keogh sums per-point multichannel deviations outside the allowed envelope and divides by the maximum possible path length, remaining below normalized DTW.

Differential evidence: Synthetic family and overlap-heavy tests return identical IDs and distances for brute force, default pruning and stronger LB pruning. On current 252-bar oracle cases, default zero-DTW pruning returned identical rankings: NSE pruned 212/304 and reduced 3.30 to 2.38 seconds in the first run; NASDAQ pruned 120/224 and changed 3.44 to 3.26 seconds. The stronger LB version reduced exact DTWs to 60/304 on NSE and 94/224 on NASDAQ.

Rejected default: Eager/automatic LB_Keogh was not consistently faster. In a later concurrent run it improved NSE 3.35→2.07 seconds but slowed NASDAQ 2.47→2.92 seconds. It remains opt-in; the zero-DTW bound is the default until a measured policy justifies LB overhead.

Limits: This is a correctness-first implementation over an already materialized candidate list. It has not yet run differential checks over all 24 Gate 09 cases, does not avoid raw candidate representation construction, and is not yet wired into the user-facing search command.

L-012 — Gate 11B full sampled-oracle differential

What: Added a reusable verify-pruning command, immutable gate record and readable HTML report. It reconstructs every candidate in each persisted Gate 09 case, runs the default non-DTW partial bound and the optional symmetric multivariate LB_Keogh bound, and compares both outputs with the stored exhaustive authority.

Why: Two real examples and synthetic tests were insufficient to call pruning exact-safe. The optimization must preserve ordered episode IDs, total distance and every explainable component across markets, lookbacks, historical/current cutoffs, quality tiers and liquidity strata.

How: Candidate episode IDs and windows are reconstructed from the locked oracle Parquets and source data. The verifier uses the same 60-session gap, overlap deduplication, per-instrument cap and top-k as the oracle. A case passes only when ordered IDs are identical and totals/components agree within 1e-12. It separately records eligible count, exact evaluations, safe prunes, bound evaluations and scoring time.

Correctness evidence: NSE passed 12/12 cases over 3,192 candidates; NASDAQ passed 12/12 over 2,658. Both modes reproduced all 24 oracle rankings. Maximum total-distance delta was 4.440892098500626e-16; maximum component delta was zero.

Work avoided: Default pruning evaluated 1,045/3,192 NSE candidates and 966/2,658 NASDAQ candidates, safely avoiding 67.26% and 63.66% of exact evaluations. LB_Keogh evaluated 781 and 841, avoiding 75.53% and 68.36%.

Performance decision: Default aggregate scoring took 23.39 s NSE and 22.11 s NASDAQ. LB_Keogh took 22.73 s and 22.46 s. The stronger bound reduces exact DTW work but is not consistently faster, so D-008 keeps it opt-in.

Regression evidence: The complete suite passes 72/72 tests in 25.39 seconds; measured source coverage is 88%. Both generated Gate 11B JSON records parse successfully, both HTML reports were written, documentation HTML parses, and git diff --check is clean.

Limits: The verifier measures the scorer after candidate representations are materialized. User-facing search still uses the older exact reranker, and the complete exchange is not an exhaustive exact candidate set. Next integrate persisted view retrieval with the verified pruned reranker, then build an independent Gate 12 matrix.

L-013 — Gate 11C production retrieval integration

What: Connected persisted signature retrieval to exact representation materialization and the verified Gate 11B pruned reranker in the user-facing search --view-store path. Added an independent verify-production-search command, machine gate and readable repeated-run report.

Why: Separate candidate and pruning verifiers do not prove their production handoff. The command must retain time/overlap/tier filters, detect stale source data, preserve deterministic results, expose stage costs and fail visibly rather than silently search an incomplete store.

How: Search validates manifest dataset, schema, signature/representation versions, digest and incomplete-build state; every loaded shard is content-checked. Hits carry source fingerprint, quality tier and representation provenance. Before exact work, current source fingerprints and quality tiers are checked for every eligible shard, not merely selected hits: otherwise a changed non-selected symbol could wrongly remain outside the pool. Stored episode IDs are reconstructed and verified, representations are materialized deterministically in serial or thread-pool order, then exact-safe pruning runs. HTML provenance separates candidate, fingerprint, materialization and exact stages.

Adversarial tests: Serial and parallel materialization return identical IDs; a Parquet file mutated after store construction is rejected with an explicit rebuild instruction; command-level composition writes exact/pruning provenance; repeated verifier digests and every total/component distance must agree.

Late correctness finding and repair: The first integration version validated stock files and tiers but could not detect a changed benchmark index, even though market-context signatures depend on it. Shard schema v3 therefore adds the benchmark content fingerprint to every shard and the manifest. Both full stores were rebuilt atomically rather than claiming old shards were current. A deliberate post-build benchmark mutation now fails. This supersedes the earlier v2 performance measurements while preserving them as history.

Schema-v3 store evidence: NSE rebuilt 1,843 shards in 101.60 s and then reused all in 8.59 s; manifest digest eee238cec7556a0894267e1ccbe6465b0ea01c8858b598e867d343cbe356c157. NASDAQ rebuilt 11,584 shards in 488.95 s and reused all in 29.80 s; digest 760efe9c0f9e29d76670b44a035b95951155aca44c8234579a90beb4d4509891. Both have the same row/quality counts as v2 and zero failures.

NSE final evidence: Two schema-v3 252-bar RELIANCE runs loaded and source/quality/benchmark-validated all 1,843 shards, then considered 1,040,117 eligible rows. Ordered result digest 0badb2446edfcdaec2da97f6551324b8959c8583ad845511d3ca22aa3928ed4a repeated exactly with zero numeric delta. Worst end-to-end engine time was 31.54 s, peak RSS 484.41 MB, maximum candidate stage 17.47 s, fingerprint validation 0.56 s, materialization 11.59 s and exact scoring 1.90 s. Default pruning skipped 97/175 exact candidates (55.43%).

NASDAQ final evidence: Two schema-v3 AAPL runs validated all 11,584 shards and considered 3,786,118 eligible rows. Digest 371e51e49ae7934bc8ae580b8439787fc67169c6dc344a143136bf053681e26c repeated exactly with zero numeric delta. Worst engine time was 84.73 s, peak RSS 776.00 MB, candidate stage 68.60 s, fingerprint validation 2.92 s, materialization 12.34 s and exact scoring 1.54 s. Default pruning skipped 88/175 candidates (50.29%).

Gate decision: Both markets pass the declared 252-bar smoke budgets (NSE ≤120 s, NASDAQ ≤300 s, RSS ≤1 GiB). Keep this backend opt-in under D-009: one current query per exchange is an integration/performance proof, not independent full-universe recall validation.

Limits and next step: Only lookback 252 has complete persisted stores. Gate 12 must select new symbols, historical/current cutoffs and difficult regimes not used for pool calibration; 63/126 stores require an explicit disk/storage plan before construction.

Regression evidence: The final suite passes 76/76 tests, including stock, quality-tier and benchmark staleness failures. A targeted pytest-cov invocation exposed a NumPy/SciPy module-reload collection error in the coverage tooling; the normal regression suite is unaffected, so no new coverage percentage is claimed at this checkpoint.

L-014 — Constrained-pruning threshold correction

Finding: Planning an exhaustive Gate 12 reference exposed an adversarial case absent from the 24 sampled oracles. Suppose A and B initially fill top-2. A later, cheaper C overlaps both, so greedy overlap selection now contains only C and requires farther non-overlapping D. The scorer recomputed the selected set but retained the old A/B finite threshold when the set became incomplete; D could therefore be stopped even though it was required.

Fix: After every exact evaluation, set the bound threshold to the worst selected distance only when the constrained result contains top_k matches. If overlap deduplication or an instrument cap leaves fewer than top_k, reopen the threshold to infinity until the result is full again. This preserves the safe stopping proof: every candidate capable of changing the complete constrained prefix has been evaluated before a finite frontier can stop the scan.

Regression: Added the explicit A/B/C/D overlap topology. The old implementation would return only C; the corrected scorer evaluates D and returns C,D. This test is independent of financial feature values and directly exercises the control-flow invariant.

Gate 11B rerun: All 12 NSE and 12 NASDAQ exact-oracle cases still pass with maximum total delta 4.44e-16 and zero component delta. Work counts are unchanged: default prunes 2,147/3,192 NSE and 1,692/2,658 NASDAQ candidates; optional LB_Keogh prunes 2,411 and 1,817.

Gate 11C rerun: Schema-v3 RELIANCE digest remains 0badb2446edfcdaec2da97f6551324b8959c8583ad845511d3ca22aa3928ed4a; worst repeated time 29.05 s and peak 476.02 MB. AAPL remains 371e51e49ae7934bc8ae580b8439787fc67169c6dc344a143136bf053681e26c; worst 76.23 s and 772.26 MB. Both have zero repeat delta and the same 97/175 and 88/175 prune counts.

Decision: Supersede the incomplete threshold rule described in L-011 with the reopen-on-incomplete invariant above. Keep the broader Gate 12 claim open: passing known cases did not substitute for adversarial reasoning here.

L-015 — Gate 12 exhaustive-reference plan and independent registry rule

What: Added a task-by-task Gate 12 HTML plan covering registry freeze, exact-feature feasibility, vectorized exact lower bounds, a two-pass exhaustive constrained authority, scale ladder, independent validation, lookback/disk expansion and promotion.

Why: Pool 175 passes sampled Gate 09 recall and Gate 11C integration, but the complete exchange still has no exact authority. Pool 1,000 and persisted signatures are approximations, not proof that no omitted episode is closer.

Feasibility finding: The exact representation needs approximately 1,353 numeric values per episode. For 4,884,685 current 252-bar rows, raw storage projects to 26.4 GB float32 or 13.2 GB float16 before metadata, while only 9.7 GB is free. Float16 is not accepted as an exact authority. The plan therefore gates any storage attempt and prefers a two-pass compact lower-bound frontier.

Independent selection: Seed gate12-query-v1; exclude all Gate 09 query and candidate symbols; require ≥1,000 rows; select one SHA-256 winner from every A/B × low/middle/high liquidity cell per market before recall. Locked NSE symbols: homefirst, vinylindia, shrirampps, indiaglyco, aceinteg, onwardtec. Locked NASDAQ: CAKE, ZICA, SLE, TVTX, ABEOW, THRD_old. Current and deterministic 70%-history cutoffs produce 24 planned 252-bar cases.

Decision: Implement T12-00 registry persistence next. Do not build 63/126 stores or promote persisted search by default until the exact-reference feasibility and independent 252-bar matrix pass.

Readable plan: Gate 12 exhaustive reference and independent validation.

L-016 — T12-00 deterministic independent query registry

What: Added a market-agnostic Gate 12 registry generator, validator, CLI command, machine gate and YAML/Parquet/HTML artifacts. The registry records explicit query cutoffs/positions, episode IDs, quality/liquidity cell, source and benchmark fingerprints, exclusion-set digest, selection hash and whole-registry digest.

Why: Independent validation queries must be frozen before recall and must not overlap the Gate 09 queries or sampled candidate instruments used while choosing pool 175. A readable symbol list alone does not detect later source, benchmark, quality, cutoff or registry changes.

Rejected v1: The first deterministic run selected ZICA, ABEOW and THRD_old for NASDAQ low/middle cells, but their latest observations were 2009, 2019 and 2014. No Gate 12 recall had been computed. Calling those “current” would be misleading, so schema v1 was rejected visibly and the pre-registration rule was versioned before validation.

v2 rule: In addition to ≥1,000 rows and Gate 09 exclusion, require the last bar to be within 120 calendar days of the market’s latest audited timestamp. This leaves NSE unchanged and selects active TAIT, YGMZ and RKDA for those NASDAQ cells. A future data refresh may invalidate the fingerprinted registry; it must not silently select replacements after recall exists.

Evidence: NSE: 26 excluded symbols, 12/12 cases, registry digest 1d31b954f43f56b1883ba3f92d3195dc99e41fc3522587f9696c39302b7656dd, exclusion digest cce8289007953e524ef8bda9c39eaf852239c7d7bfc3e30b9c431e37fdb6318c. NASDAQ: 20 excluded, 12/12 cases, registry d2106561e356dadd38f35003cfed27cbd0a3a5990c77adb59b213fe8b55be43e, exclusion 6f03768f7ee319a414232c487c6e0672eb78a0adf6100097fa60e7f6ed573c5a. Both validators return zero failures from saved YAML.

Tests: Liquidity-row shuffling preserves registry digest; all six cells and two cutoffs exist; Gate 09 symbols are absent; historical cases retain ≥60 future sessions; stock mutation, benchmark mutation, quality-tier drift, cutoff/episode tampering and exclusion/registry digest mismatch fail. T12-01 is next; no recall has been inspected.

L-017 — T12-01 exact-storage feasibility and architecture lock

What: Added a market-agnostic feasibility verifier, CLI command, machine gate and HTML report. It samples deterministic windows from all 12 locked symbols, serializes native, float32 and float16 layouts, reloads them, and differentially checks every representation field, exact non-DTW lower bound, lower-bound components, rigid-price term, completed total/components and candidate ordering.

Why: The planning estimate treated all 1,353 numeric values as float32. Inspection and measurement show the actual exact contract is mixed precision: the 128 coarse values are float32, but 1,225 channel/stage/structural values are float64. Choosing a store from the lower estimate could silently lose exactness or exhaust disk.

How: The versioned compressed probe stores 19×48 and 4×64 optional samples with 23 presence flags, 48 stage values, 9 structural values and the 128-value coarse vector. The loader validates schema, shapes, row counts and a content digest. The CLI revalidates both frozen registries against current stock, benchmark and quality fingerprints; checks YAML/Parquet identity; verifies current view-manifest digests against their gates; and derives the complete 4,884,685-row projection from those manifests.

Evidence: Forty-eight representations (four windows for each of six NSE and six NASDAQ symbols; label digest 7f97ad7856525141622389ef00313312942852c5e4ac46e24ab07d4acdc1171a) were measured. Native is bit-exact for all fields and produces zero lower-bound, component and total delta. Its sample-compressed footprint is 9,287.58 bytes/row; with the preregistered 1.25 safety factor the store projects to 56,708,648,743 bytes (52.81 GiB). The 64-byte/row frontier adds 312,619,840 bytes (0.29 GiB).

Reduced-precision rejection: Float32 projects to 29,121,626,973 bytes (27.12 GiB) but changes fields by up to 2.84e-6, completed totals by 6.81e-8 and components by 3.03e-7. Float16 projects to 14,976,266,122 bytes (13.95 GiB) and changes totals by 4.06e-4 and components by 2.76e-3. Rankings happened to agree in this small probe, but both layouts fail the fixed 1e-12 authority requirement and cannot be promoted.

Resource decision: Free space at the final gate was 10,179,444,736 bytes (9.48 GiB), of which 5 GiB must remain reserved. A lossless store plus frontier would need 57,021,268,583 bytes. Gate 12B therefore passes by selecting two_pass_streaming, as explicitly allowed by T12-01; it does not build the infeasible store.

Tests and visible failure: Tests cover missing optional context, constant arrays, extreme finite values, native equality, reduced-precision detection, corrupt/truncated payload, version mismatch and invalid resource controls. The complete regression passes 88/88. The first real command used unavailable /usr/bin/time and exited 127 before any work; the shell-timed retry completed in 6.09 seconds. A diff audit then found that distance comparisons were concentrated at the start of the sample; before commit they were spread across positions 0,15,31,47 and eight disjoint candidates spanning both exchanges, layout validation was strengthened, and the final gate passed in 7.02 seconds. T12-02 vectorized exact lower-bound work is next; no Gate 12 recall has been measured.

L-018 — T12-02 vectorized exact lower-bound kernel

What: Added a market-independent sliding exact-representation kernel and vectorized lower-bound comparison. It reproduces the scalar 128-value float32 coarse vector, 19×48 optional samples, 4×64 DTW samples, 48 stage values, 9 directional-event structural values, missing-channel masks and every non-DTW component. Configurable chunking bounds memory without changing positions or results.

How: Sliding OHLCV windows are processed as matrices. Rolling mean/extrema and the two-stage robust median/MAD transforms operate across windows; interpolation retains the scalar missing-data rule; benchmark path/drawdown/relative channels reset causally inside each episode; stage blocks and structural directional events are evaluated in batches. Lower-bound groups use the identical pairwise robust scale, float32 coarse evaluation order and configured weights.

Correctness: Full, partial and absent benchmark context, constant price, zero volume, future benchmark mutation and whole-versus-chunked runs match scalar fields within 1e-12. Across the real gate, maximum total delta is 8.88e-16 and maximum component/rigid delta is 3.55e-15. Tests caught and corrected two float32 operation-order differences in coarse distance and an initial vectorized structural-loop axis error before the gate.

Failed performance attempt retained: The first 12-symbol run used a single cold timing. Aggregate speedup was 25.15× and RSS 352 MB, but CAKE, homefirst and onwardtec measured only 18.94×, 17.41× and 18.43×, so Gate 12C failed. The 20× threshold was not reduced.

Final benchmark protocol and evidence: Warm a ten-window batch, then use the median of three complete batch timings to reduce cold-start/noise bias; the scalar reference remains a full online representation plus lower bound for every window. All 12 frozen symbols and 1,200 windows pass individually: minimum speedup 20.5536×, aggregate 25.0741×, peak RSS 373.99 MB against the 512 MB limit. Final wall time was 61.46 seconds.

Decision: T12-02 passes. Proceed to T12-03’s resumable frontier and constrained two-pass exhaustive authority. No independent Gate 12 recall has yet been measured.

L-019 — Float16 behavioral audit and two-tier precision policy

Prompt and correction: The user challenged whether 1e-12 accuracy was necessary and asked to use float16 if it was sufficient. That challenge is correct for practical retrieval: twelve decimal places are not a meaningful product requirement. They remain valuable only for internal scalar-versus-optimized parity and safe-reference proofs.

What was tested: Added a reusable float16 precision verifier, CLI command, machine gates and HTML reports. For every Gate 09 case, keep the query native, quantize all candidate representation fields to float16, run the complete composite distance and constrained top-20 selection, then compare membership, order, graded nDCG and numeric drift against the persisted native oracle.

Evidence: NSE passes 12/12 cases and 3,192 candidates: minimum recall 1.0, minimum graded nDCG 0.9999924999, 11/12 exact orders, maximum total delta 0.0035484, component delta 0.0246707. NASDAQ passes 12/12 and 2,658 candidates: recall/nDCG 1.0, 12/12 exact orders, total delta 0.0043553, component delta 0.0190371. The sole difference swapped ranks 16/17 in TCS current 126; top-20 membership did not change.

Resource check: Float16’s safety-adjusted full-store projection remains 14,976,266,122 bytes. Adding the 312,619,840-byte frontier and 5 GiB reserve requires about 20.66 GB versus about 10.18 GB free. It does not fit even without the reserve. Thus lowering precision would not alter T12-01’s streaming decision.

Decision: Keep and explicitly endorse float16 for approximate candidate signatures—the existing view store already defaults to float16 and promotes only overflow shards to float32. Permit float16 for a future approximate cache if it passes recall 1.0 and graded nDCG ≥0.999 on untouched cases, followed by native reranking. Keep native precision for the streaming exhaustive authority because float16 changes ordering and its rounded lower bounds are not proven safe for the native metric. No user-visible report should imply that twelve-decimal distance precision is financially significant.

Readable policy: precision policy — float16 versus native.

L-020 — T12-03 resumable two-pass exhaustive authority

What: Added query-specific, per-symbol exact-lower-bound frontier shards with atomic writes, schema/batch versions, sorted episode IDs/cutoffs/bounds, content digests, stock and benchmark fingerprints, quality digest, request-scope digest and a whole-manifest digest. Resume reuses only completely valid shards. The second pass performs a k-way bound merge, reconstructs candidates with the exact batch kernel, rechecks every stored bound, completes native DTW and stops only when a full overlap/per-instrument-constrained top-k has a threshold strictly below the next global bound.

Correctness tests: Frontier results equal brute force in ordered IDs and totals; exact accounting satisfies eligible = evaluated + safely pruned; equal-distance inputs, query overlap and per-instrument caps remain deterministic; batch-size changes preserve the manifest; a missing shard rebuilds without changing its digest; truncated shards, manifest/content mismatch, source mutation, query-source mutation, benchmark drift, quality drift and request-scope drift fail visibly.

Bug found before promotion: The first 63-bar reconstruction failed because the batch lower-bound group average counted channels that were absent from both query and candidate. Their distance was correctly zero, but including them in the denominator understated the group. The comparator now tracks per-row included-channel counts exactly like the scalar scorer. New 63-bar missing-context parity passes; the frontier refuses any reconstructed bound delta above 1e-12.

Performance failure retained: The first eight-instrument NSE smoke reconstructed each globally ordered candidate through scalar pandas. It exceeded seven minutes and was interrupted during the repeated search. Atomic frontier files survived. Completion was redesigned to pull safe frontier batches, group candidates by symbol, build exact representations vectorially and cache a bounded number of shards. The same search then fell to about 31 seconds.

NSE evidence: Frozen homefirst historical 252-bar query, eight instruments considered (seven eligible shards and one quality exclusion), 4,351 eligible candidates. Fresh build 6.97 s; final unchanged resume 0.06 s and searches 30.05/30.71 s. Bounded 256-row work batches evaluate 2,027, safely prune 2,324 (53.41%), return 20 matches with zero repeat delta, maximum bound delta 4.44e-16, threshold 1.1473476, immediate next bound 1.1473569 and peak RSS 436.08 MB. Manifest 932bae85b68c7da86f8ea9a6dea1925ad1c1ba0a3ddf108a4d4f4de4f6570685.

NASDAQ scope failure and correction: The first CAKE historical smoke used eight instruments. It exhausted all 1,289 candidates correctly but could form only nine non-overlapping matches, so it failed top-20 and early-stop requirements. Expanding scope—not lowering top-k—to 20 instruments passes. The incremental expansion build took 2.90 s; the final unchanged run reuses all 20 shards in 0.17 s. It has 2,594 eligible, 2,167 evaluated, 427 pruned (16.46%), searches 30.23/29.01 s, zero repeat delta, bound delta 4.44e-16, threshold 1.5268917, next 1.5280502 and RSS 421.96 MB. Manifest aaa291521f7b06ef49e344c8c2de39a018c805fa74b1e4c6373b8cfe2ea330b7.

Final verification: The corrected exact-batch kernel passes all 12 frozen real cases and 1,200 windows at 21.17× minimum / 26.21× aggregate speedup, ≤3.55e-15 numeric parity and 341.33 MB RSS. The repository regression suite passes all 106 tests. The 1e-12 threshold is retained only as an internal native-reference parity alarm; practical approximate signatures remain float16 under the separately verified precision policy.

Decision: T12-03 architecture and bounded market-agnostic smokes pass. This is not full-exchange completeness. T12-04 must run the deterministic scale ladder, measure full Pass 1/2 resource projections and optimize if the declared NSE/NASDAQ offline budgets are not met.

L-021 — T12-04 deterministic scale ladder, NSE 1% and 10%

What: Added a reusable scale-ladder verifier and CLI. It selects nested A/B instruments by a locked SHA-256 order, supports explicit validated instrument scopes, creates separate atomic frontier roots per rung, resumes unchanged shards, searches twice, compares complete result digests, reconciles exact/pruned accounting, checks strict stopping, disk reserve and RSS, and emits both a machine gate and readable HTML report. Frontier construction now independently checks the expected stride-window count for every symbol.

Why: A complete NSE/NASDAQ run should not be started from an eight-instrument smoke. The ladder makes resource growth and failure modes visible at 1%, 10%, 50% and 100%, while keeping projections explicitly separate from complete-universe evidence.

1% NSE evidence: The frozen current homefirst query selects 19 of 1,843 eligible symbols. Fresh build 16.01 s, resume 0.08 s, 10,766 candidates, 786 exact, 9,980 safely pruned (92.69%), searches 22.35/22.57 s, identical digest c519f85b…25c3, 518.03 MB RSS and 388,103 frontier bytes. Linear projections are 25.9 minutes build and 36.1 minutes search.

Performance failure retained: The initial combined 10% run used globally interleaved 256-row work and a 32-shard cache. Although its first exact pass completed, the repeated pass was still cycling expensive symbol representations after more than 12 minutes and was interrupted. The complete 185-shard atomic frontier survived. Finite-threshold work was redesigned to drain the currently competitive prefix of one symbol at a time while preserving the global lower-bound stop rule.

Memory experiment: The corrected 10% traversal first passed at 212.87/219.45 s but retained 32 representation shards and peaked at 917.98 MB. Because symbol draining does not revisit completed shards, the scale default was reduced to two. The final rerun preserved the exact digest and accounting while reducing RSS to 509.77 MB; searches were 219.24/230.95 s.

10% NSE evidence: The locked prefix contains 185 symbols and 104,396 candidates. Only 906 require native completion; 103,490 are safely pruned (99.13%). Both runs return digest d8b0f8c9…d347. The conservative full-search projection is 2,184.07 s (36.4 minutes), below the two-hour offline limit; disk reserve passes. The build is resume-only in the final certificate, so no fresh-build projection is claimed from that run.

Regression: Brute-force differentials pass after the traversal change, including one-row discovery batches, ties, overlaps and per-symbol caps. Refreshed T12-03 NSE/NASDAQ smokes retain their thresholds, deterministic results and ≤4.44e-16 bound parity.

Decision: T12-04 remains incomplete. The 50% and 100% NSE rungs and 1%/10% NASDAQ rungs must pass before T12-05. The next NSE rung is allowed by the 10% time/RSS/disk evidence, but its repeated native run is long and must remain an explicit measured task.

L-022 — T12-04 NSE 50% exact scale rung

Scope: Ran the frozen current homefirst query over the first 50% of the locked gate12-scale-v1 SHA-256 order: 922 of 1,843 eligible A/B symbols. No query, distance, precision, top-k, gap, cache or coverage control changed from the accepted ladder policy.

Pass 1: A fresh atomic frontier built all 922 shards in 827.66 seconds (13.79 minutes). The unchanged resume revalidated them in 1.93 seconds. The frontier contains 526,248 eligible candidate rows and occupies 18,885,389 bytes; the full projection is about 37.7 MB, so the 5 GiB disk reserve passes comfortably.

Pass 2: The native exhaustive authority evaluates 900 candidates and safely prunes 525,348 (99.83%). Searches complete in 571.31 and 579.00 seconds and return the identical digest c0781c24…cabf. Peak RSS is 564.06 MB. Exact/pruned accounting reconciles, the selected top-20 is full, and the next-bound stopping certificate passes.

Projection: Scaling the measured 50% work by the exact eligible-symbol ratio projects 1,654.43 seconds (27.6 minutes) for a fresh full build and 1,141.99 seconds (19.0 minutes) for a full exact search. Both are below the two-hour NSE offline limit. Projections remain planning evidence; only the actual 100% rung can establish complete NSE authority.

Decision: The 100% NSE rung is authorized by time, RSS and disk evidence. T12-04 is still open until that rung and the NASDAQ 1%/10% rungs pass.

L-023 — Nested frontier reuse before the NSE 100% rung

Why: Building the 100% scale root independently would recompute the already certified 922-symbol half-universe and add roughly 14 minutes without increasing correctness evidence.

How: Before a larger rung, the ladder locates the largest completed lower-scale root for the same query and hard-links its per-symbol NPZ shards into the new root. The normal exhaustive builder then validates every seeded shard against the new explicit instrument scope, current source and benchmark fingerprints, quality/request provenance, schema and content digest. Invalid shards are not accepted; atomic replacement in the larger root cannot alter the lower-rung directory entry.

Reporting: Each rung now records seeded, newly built and reused instrument counts. A mixed seeded build deliberately omits a fresh-full-build projection because its elapsed time measures only incremental work. Frontier byte projections remain logical and therefore conservative even where hard links avoid physical duplication.

Tests: A nested 50%/100% fixture proves the full rung seeds two verified shards, reuses both, builds the remaining eligible shard, repeats the same exact digest and reconciles candidate accounting. All 108 repository tests pass.

Decision: Proceed to the actual 100% NSE rung using the locked two-shard completion cache and the existing 50% certificate as the validated seed.

L-024 — Complete NSE exhaustive authority and ladder aggregation

Scope: Ran the frozen current homefirst query over every one of the 1,843 eligible A/B NSE symbols in the locked hash universe. The full frontier contains 1,040,116 temporally eligible, non-query-overlapping stride-five episodes.

Build: Seeded and revalidated all 922 certified 50% shards, then built the remaining 921 atomically in 846.93 seconds. An unchanged complete resume validates all shards in 3.35 seconds. Logical frontier size is 37,381,718 bytes and free disk remains 9,369,423,872 bytes, above the 5 GiB reserve.

Exact result: Only 1,278 candidates require native completion; 1,038,838 are safely pruned (99.88%). Independent searches take 913.82 and 950.78 seconds and return identical digest 4149ea4b…e4e1. Peak RSS is 638.14 MB. Exact/pruned accounting, full constrained top-20, bound reconstruction and strict next-bound stopping all pass.

Bookkeeping correction: Individual rung invocations originally updated a generic smoke gate, while immutable history correctly retained 1%, 10%, 50% and 100%. Added a reproducible aggregate command that accepts only passing rungs with the same dataset, frozen query, locked seed and identical repeated digest, keeps the newest valid record per fraction, and emits the full gate only when every preregistered fraction exists. 12e_exhaustive_scale_full_nse now passes with all four rungs.

Tests: History aggregation rejects failed, mismatched and nondeterministic records and selects the newest matching passing rung. Parser coverage and the full repository suite now pass 109 tests.

Decision: The NSE portion of T12-04 is complete. This is one full exact reference query, not the 24-case recall matrix. Proceed to NASDAQ 1% and 10%; do not start T12-05 until both pass their six-hour, 1 GiB and disk gates.

L-025 — T12-04 NASDAQ 1% scale rung

Scope: Ran the frozen current CAKE query over the first 1% of the locked NASDAQ hash universe: 116 of 11,584 eligible A/B symbols and 39,160 stride-five candidate episodes.

Evidence: Fresh build completes in 70.34 seconds and unchanged resume in 0.38 seconds. The exact authority evaluates 1,005 and safely prunes 38,155 (97.43%). Searches take 97.13/98.70 seconds and return identical digest 8afabf1c…08e7. Peak RSS is 486.95 MB, frontier size is 1,520,272 bytes, accounting and strict stopping pass, and free disk remains above the 5 GiB reserve.

Projection: Scaling by the exact eligible-symbol ratio projects 7,023.99 seconds (1.95 h) for a fresh full build and 9,699.62 seconds (2.69 h) for one full exact search. Both are below the preregistered six-hour NASDAQ per-phase limit, but remain estimates until a larger rung confirms them.

Decision: Authorize the nested 10% NASDAQ rung. It must reuse/revalidate these 116 shards, build the remaining locked prefix, repeat its exact digest and remain below six hours, 1 GiB RSS and the disk reserve before T12-04 can close.

L-026 — T12-04 NASDAQ 10% and scale-ladder completion

Scope: Ran the frozen current CAKE query over 1,159 of 11,584 eligible NASDAQ symbols, the complete locked 10% hash prefix. The root seeded and revalidated all 116 passing 1% shards and built the remaining 1,043.

Evidence: Incremental build takes 563.82 seconds and unchanged resume 1.70 seconds. The frontier contains 363,633 eligible candidates and occupies 14,314,216 logical bytes. Native completion evaluates 817 and safely prunes 362,816 (99.78%). Searches take 409.18/395.53 seconds and return identical digest dc7883c2…451e. Peak RSS is 546.73 MB; disk reserve, accounting, bound reconstruction, full constrained top-20 and strict stopping all pass.

Projection: The exact symbol-ratio projection from 10% is 4,089.73 seconds (68.2 minutes) for one full NASDAQ search. The final build is mixed seeded/incremental, so no misleading fresh-full-build projection is emitted. The earlier fresh 1% build projects 1.95 hours, also within six hours.

Aggregate: 12e_exhaustive_scale_full_nasdaq accepts the immutable 1% and 10% records for the same frozen query and seed and passes. Together with 12e_exhaustive_scale_full_nse, all T12-04 preregistered scale rungs now pass.

Decision: T12-04 is complete. Proceed to T12-05, but first plan multi-query execution carefully: 24 independent exhaustive authorities are intentionally expensive, and the validation pool must remain frozen while references are generated and checkpointed.

L-027 — T12-05 blind, resumable authority contract

What: Added a standalone exact-authority module plus build-gate12-authority and aggregate-gate12-authorities commands. Each frozen case produces an atomic JSON authority and a readable HTML certificate. A valid completed artifact is the restart checkpoint, so expensive exact work is not repeated.

Why: The 24 validation queries must remain genuinely untouched. Exact answers need to be generated and frozen before production pool recall or forward outcomes are inspected, while multi-hour market runs must survive interruption without accepting partial output.

Integrity: The artifact binds the frozen registry digest, query and request, query-source and benchmark fingerprints, exact frontier manifest, build/resume provenance, complete ranked match IDs/distances/components/alignments, repeated result and both search certificates. Content and result digests expose mutation. Candidate accounting must reconcile. The proof accepts strict next-bound stopping or, for a universe with nothing left to prune, literal full exhaustion; ambiguous partial completion fails.

Reuse: A matching scale-ladder frontier may seed a case through hard links or copies, but every shard is revalidated by the normal frontier builder against the current source, benchmark, quality, request, schema and content digest. Other queries build independent query-specific shards.

Verification: Round-trip, candidate-universe and registry provenance drift, tamper detection, full-exhaustion certificate, scale-frontier seeding, completed-checkpoint resume, HTML output and existing exhaustive/brute-force differentials pass. The complete repository suite passes 111 tests. Authority generation is now ready; no case authority or recall result is claimed by this implementation checkpoint.

Next: Generate and freeze all 12 NSE and 12 NASDAQ authorities, checkpointing case by case. Aggregate both matrices only after all artifacts validate, and only then run the preregistered production pool recall comparison.

L-028 — First frozen full-universe authority: NSE homefirst current

Scope: Generated the first T12-05 reference for nse-homefirst-current-252 / episode 99fe49f51285b398361b7b13. The run read neither production retrieval output nor forward outcomes.

L-031 — Unattended sequential authority-matrix supervisor

What: Added run-gate12-authority-matrix to execute the frozen registry autonomously, one case process at a time: NSE first, then its 12/12 aggregate, NASDAQ second, then its aggregate. The durable JSON progress checkpoint records current/completed/failed cases, timestamps, episode IDs, exit codes and free disk before and after each case.

Why: The 24 exhaustive references are intentionally expensive and should continue without requiring a user message between cases. A separate process per case releases memory and isolates failures while retaining the existing atomic frontier and authority restart behavior.

Guards: Every listed registry must contain exactly 12 unique cases. The supervisor invokes the normal provenance-validating authority command even for an existing artifact; therefore it skips expensive work only after current query/request/registry/source-universe/benchmark integrity passes. It stops on the first nonzero case or aggregate result and before starting any case if free disk is below the 5 GiB reserve. It never invokes production recall or outcome analysis.

Verification: Tests prove NSE-before-NASDAQ ordering across 24 cases, one aggregate per completed dataset, durable completion records and immediate stop with later cases pending on the first simulated failure. The complete repository suite passes 113 tests.

Operation: Launch the supervisor with stdout/stderr redirected to a durable log. The first three authorities will be cheaply revalidated, then execution resumes at nse-vinylindia-historical-252. Generated frontier, authority, progress and run-log artifacts remain outside Git; human evidence continues in this append-only log at measured checkpoints.

L-032 — Autonomous 24-case matrix execution launched

Launch: Started the persistent sequential supervisor on 19 August 2026 after commit 9e79dff and a 113-test full regression pass. Output is tee’d to gate12/authorities/matrix-runner.log; atomic machine progress is stored in gate12/authorities/matrix-runner.json.

It then automatically started case 4/24, nse-vinylindia-historical-252. At the first operational checkpoint its child process was live, 148 query-specific shards were durable, approximately 8.75 GiB remained free and no failure was recorded.

Continuation policy: No user message is required between cases. After each successful artifact, progress is atomically advanced and the next registry case starts in a fresh process. A failing case, failed 12-case aggregate, or free disk below 5 GiB stops the sequence visibly; it does not skip ahead or reduce scope.

L-033 — External Kullamagi labelled-example morphology audit

What: Added a reproducible command that downloads the published Kullamagi positions sheet, normalizes only explicit setup aliases, constructs 252-session episodes ending at the previous completed session, and compares each example with exact composite distance. It writes the immutable source snapshot, coverage table, neighbour table, machine metrics and a readable HTML report.

Why: These third-party tracker labels can test whether the representation groups trader-recognized chart morphologies without pretending the labels are inputs to the matcher, official Kullamagi ground truth, or subjective human verification.

Causal controls: Spreadsheet result and chart-after-exit fields never enter similarity. The entry-day daily bar is excluded because its close and final volume were not available at an intraday entry. Same-symbol candidates are excluded. The primary mode permits only labelled episodes whose cutoff is at least 60 observed query sessions earlier; the all-date retrospective mode is diagnostic because same-date sector peers inflate it.

Coverage: The downloaded snapshot contains 486 parsed rows and 180 unique symbols. The present NASDAQ source provides 132 deduplicated episodes across 56 symbols; 319 rows have no source symbol, 30 lack 252 pre-entry sessions and five duplicate an existing symbol/cutoff. This is venue-, delisting- and survivorship-limited coverage, not a complete audit of the sheet.

Measured result: The primary causal set has 73 queries. Top-1 setup agreement is 52.05% versus a 41.41% within-query random-candidate expectation and is significant under 1,000 random candidate draws (p=0.0190), but remains below a naive 67.12% always-breakout classifier. Top-5 purity is 51.51% versus the same 41.41% expectation (p=0.0010). Breakouts score 71.43% top-1 and parabolics 50%; all six breakdowns, eight episodic pivots and three mean-reversion examples miss at top-1. The honest conclusion is measurable neighborhood structure dominated by common setups, not reliable named-pattern recovery.

Verification: A synthetic in-memory regression asserts previous-session cutoffs, dynamic observed-session gap naming, cross-symbol-only candidates, outcome blindness and generic top-k reporting. The random-candidate null samples from each query's own eligible set, preserving its temporal restriction and available setup mix. Parser alias tests and CLI registration pass. The complete repository suite passes 116/116 tests; exact real-data reruns complete 2,179 pairs in 50.93–64.56 seconds under concurrent Gate 12 load with no operational failure. The sheet's live-price cells change its raw byte hash, while the canonical entry-date/symbol/side/setup digest remains stable.

Next: Preserve this as a baseline. Add historical NYSE/ETF/delisted coverage before drawing broader label conclusions, then test each labelled query against the frozen full historical universe rather than only other sheet examples. Any metric/channel adjustment must use a training split and be judged on a locked later split to avoid fitting these 2021 labels.

L-035 — Yahoo-expanded tracker audit and copyable Excel workbook

Request: Replace the NASDAQ-only coverage limitation by fetching all symbols and trade dates from the published Kullamagi positions tracker through yfinance, declare 75% setup purity as the desired threshold, and provide a workbook that can be copied and inspected.

Implementation: Added an optional external-data dependency group with yfinance 1.5.2 and openpyxl, a cached Yahoo Parquet source, class-share symbol normalization, batched downloads plus individual omission retries, SPY benchmark context, provenance manifest, provider coverage export and analyze-kullamagi-yfinance command. Data uses split-adjusted daily OHLCV from 26 December 2018 through 31 December 2021. Unsupported/delisted tickers remain explicit; none is silently replaced by an acquirer.

Workbook: Generated kullamagi-pattern-analysis.xlsx with Summary, Query Summary, Top1 Matches, Causal TopK, Setup Performance, Trade Coverage and Yahoo Coverage sheets. It contains all 205 causally evaluable queries, 1,025 ranked matches, component distances, setup/side agreement flags, clickable entry-chart URLs, provider failures and the declared target result. Each top five contains distinct candidate symbols; repeated overlapping trades from one ticker cannot inflate purity.

Coverage: Yahoo returns history for 147/180 requested symbols. After requiring 252 sessions before entry and deduplicating identical symbol/cutoff episodes, 361 examples across 129 symbols are usable—2.7× the prior 132-example NASDAQ audit. The remaining tracker rows comprise 66 unavailable-symbol rows, 36 insufficient-history rows and 23 duplicate symbol/cutoff rows.

Primary result: The 75% target is not reached. Across 205 queries with candidates at least 60 observed sessions earlier, top-1 setup agreement is 51.22% and is indistinguishable from random (p=0.451). Top-5 purity is 54.44% versus a 50.44% within-query distinct-symbol random expectation (p=0.003). This is a small statistically detectable neighborhood effect, not the requested confidence level.

Pattern breakdown: Breakout top-5 purity is 71.95%, breakdown 11.33%, episodic pivot 3.33%, parabolic 5.71%, and mean reversion 3.33%. Eighty-five of 205 individual queries reach at least 80% top-5 purity, and all 85 are breakout queries; this does not establish balanced pattern recovery. Retrospective all-date breakout purity reaches 79.53%, but same-date/sector peers and future examples make that diagnostic, not causal evidence.

Interpretation: The broader result preserves only a modest top-five signal and shows that the earlier partial-source estimate overstated its magnitude. The representation describes pre-entry development through the previous close. Episodic pivots and other trigger-defined setups depend heavily on entry-day gap, partial-session price and relative-volume pace, which are intentionally absent from this daily pre-entry audit.

Performance and integrity: The corrected low-priority real run completes 9,342 exact pairs in 431.40 seconds while Gate 12 runs concurrently. The spreadsheet's live-price formulas change the raw snapshot hash, but the canonical date/symbol/side/setup digest remains stable. Outcomes never enter similarity. All 118 repository tests pass; new tests cover Yahoo normalization/cache artifacts, benchmark loading, workbook sheet structure, causal cutoffs, distinct-symbol candidates and outcome blindness.

Next: Do not tune this 2021 audit toward 75%. Specify a trigger-aware representation—previous-close state plus entry-open gap and, where independently available, point-in-time intraday volume pace—then freeze a development/test split before measuring it. The existing pre-entry result remains the baseline.

L-036 — Case-based market memory product and verification plan

What: Added a gated plan for turning chart retrieval into a causal historical evidence system: observe a chart at a declared timestamp, retrieve diverse earlier states, explain the similarity, replay every subsequent path, and summarize outcomes with uncertainty, counterexamples, effective sample size, novelty and explicit abstention.

Why: Named setup classification is not the objective. The useful question is whether structurally similar prior market states exist and what happened next—including failures—without claiming that the present must repeat history. Professional chart study motivates case-based recall, but the computerized version must add causality, reproducibility and falsification.

How: The plan separates multi-resolution price, volume, volatility, relative-strength, structural and market-context views; keeps structure-first, full-context and shape-only retrieval visible; embargoes analogue outcomes that were not fully observable at the historical query date; prevents event/symbol duplication from manufacturing sample size; and locks proper-scoring, calibration and baseline comparisons before a final walk-forward test.

Verification sequence: M00 freezes the causality/product contract; M01–M03 verify data, chart state and unnamed synthetic structure; M04 completes exhaustive retrieval; M05–M06 build outcome replay and evidence aggregation; M07 falsifies the system against simple baselines; M08–M09 expose auditable evidence cards and freeze them in shadow operation. Every stage has an explicit correctness/performance gate and may abstain or fail.

Current operation: This plan does not promote the existing matcher. At this checkpoint the unattended Gate 12 supervisor has sealed 19/24 authorities with no failures, all NSE cases and the NSE aggregate pass, and NASDAQ TVTX historical is case 20. Approximately 5.8 GiB is free against the 5 GiB start guard, so the existing supervisor—not this document—decides whether later cases can safely begin.

Readable plan: Case-Based Market Memory implementation and verification plan.

L-037 — M00 frozen case-memory and causality contract

Locked scope: The primary product observes completed daily bars after the official close for a discretionary long-side swing horizon. Entry-open and intraday modes remain disabled. The state requires 252/126/63/21/10/5-session views. Primary evidence shows 20 neighbours with at most one contribution per symbol and event cluster; same-symbol memory is separate.

Locked outcome: The primary descriptive event is first touch of +2 ATR versus −1 ATR within 20 observed sessions using a 20-session simple-mean true range. A daily bar touching both is ambiguous and excluded. Secondary horizons are 5/10/20/40/60/126 sessions. Outcomes attach only after retrieval freezes and can contribute to a historical query only when their completion timestamp was already observable; incomplete futures are censored.

Abstention and claims: Insufficient effective sample size, novelty, unstable neighbours, contradictory context, poor data and failed calibration are mandatory reasons to abstain. The contract forbids guaranteed direction, recommendations and calibrated-probability language before M07 passes.

Generated artifacts stay outside Git; the source contract and trial ledger are versioned.

L-040 — M03 v1 unnamed structural retrieval: locked failure

What: Added eight unnamed deterministic latent OHLCV families, an independent generator oracle, scale/time/noise positives, decisive tail/context negatives, a causal masked six-resolution distance, strict verifier/config digests, CLI artifacts and an append-only trial record. The distance never receives the latent family ID and no real forward outcome is accessed.

Protocol: Candidate seeds 0–7, development-smoke seeds 100–107 and locked unseen validation seeds 200–207 are disjoint. The first registration was visibly superseded before execution because it omitted the distance-contract digest. The corrected digest 5a59236f…8046bf was then executed exactly once.

Result: The new distance achieved 96.875% overall top-1, 95.3125% overall precision@5 and zero critical-negative errors. Seven of eight hidden families cleared every structural threshold. Family latent-04 reached the 75% top-1 floor but only 65% precision@5, below the locked 75% requirement. Runtime was 65.06 seconds against 60 seconds; peak RSS was 222.55 MB against 1,024 MB. The legacy comparator had 100% top-1 and 97.5% precision@5 overall but a 15.625% critical-negative error, so its high family recognition does not make it structurally safe.

Why it failed: The retained query evidence shows the deep-decline/recovery family interleaving with the shallower decline/recovery family in four top-five neighborhoods. This is a morphology-separation failure, not a naming problem. The five-second runtime miss is also real and is not waived.

Artifacts: Readable report config/data/analogues/m03-latent-structures/m03-latent-structural-verification.html; machine result m03-latent-structural-verification.json; distance contract state-distance-contract.json; immutable gate history under gates/history/m03_latent_structural_retrieval. Next: Do not alter or rerun v1. Add a principled topology/alignment distinction and remove avoidable comparator cost using only disjoint development data, freeze it as M03b, and run a new untouched validation set exactly once. M04 remains blocked until a structural version passes.

L-041 — M03b topology-aware unnamed structural retrieval: pass

Correction: Preserved V1 unchanged and added a versioned distance whose score is 80% frozen V1 plus 20% label-blind topology. The topology term compares 252/126/63-session close-path drawdown depth, trough/deepest-drawdown timing, terminal recovery and gradients smoothed at 1/3/5 samples. No generator family ID, trader name or outcome enters the calculation.

Development evidence: The blend was selected before official execution using disjoint query seeds 100–107 and 400–407. On the full development block 400–407, V2 scored 100% top-1, 99.375% precision@5 and zero critical errors in 33.54 seconds; V1 scored 96.875%/95.9375%. Family 04 improved from 75%/70% to 100%/95%. Two-family unit seeds 700–704 were also declared observed.

Preregistration: Before generating an official query, the ledger froze verifier digest 06b17330…7245, distance digest 4771d409…65d3, prior observed seeds, untouched seeds 600–607, per-family 75% floors, 2% critical-error ceiling, 60-second runtime and 1,024-MB memory limits. M00 passed with this registration.

Single official result: All eight hidden families reached 100% top-1, 100% precision@5 and zero critical-negative errors. V1 on the identical unseen queries scored 95.3125% top-1 and precision@5; its family 04 result was 62.5%/72.5%. M03b completed in 33.7086 seconds at 215.80 MB. Neither distance saw labels, and no real forward outcomes were accessed. Direct tests also preserve distance symmetry, exact future-data causality and price/volume unit invariance under a practical 1e-6 numerical tolerance (measured scaled-copy distance 1.23e-7).

Artifacts: Readable report config/data/analogues/m03b-latent-structures/m03b-latent-structural-verification.html; machine SHA-256 7b1ad08a…1cf4c; gate SHA-256 845d40df…f3cb; query-result digest 750eb0a0…be33. Registration and completion are separate immutable ledger events.
